## File: README.md

- Extension: .md
- Language: markdown
- Size: 3136 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-23 19:43:41
- Modified: 2026-06-23 19:43:41

### Code

```markdown
# Intelligent Candidate Discovery & Ranking

Rank the **top 100** best-fit candidates out of **100,000** for one role —
**Senior AI Engineer — Founding Team** at Redrob AI — and output a CSV.

> **Strategy:** filter first, rank second. Only ~726 of 100K candidates even have an
> AI/ML title. Hard-filter the obvious rejects with cheap rules, then score the
> survivors with a **transparent weighted feature model** where embedding similarity
> is ONE feature (~18%), not the backbone. See [docs/implementation_plan.md](docs/implementation_plan.md).

---

## Reproduce the submission (the graded ≤5-min step)

```bash
# 1. Create env & install (CPU-only torch)
python -m venv .venv
. .venv/Scripts/activate            # Windows;  source .venv/bin/activate on Linux/Mac
pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu
pip install -r requirements.txt

# 2. (offline, once) cache the model + build embeddings — NO time limit
python -m src.data.embedder --cache
python src/precompute.py --candidates ./data/candidates.jsonl

# 3. Produce the submission CSV — CPU, no network, <=5 min
python src/rank.py --candidates ./data/candidates.jsonl --out ./output/<participant_id>.csv

# 4. Validate before uploading
python scripts/validate_submission.py ./output/<participant_id>.csv
```

> **torch note:** `requirements.txt` pins `torch` but the CPU build must come from the
> PyTorch CPU index (step 1 line). On a fresh machine, install torch first from that
> index, then the rest, so you don't pull a CUDA build.

---

## Repository layout

```
src/            ranking logic
  rank.py         MAIN ENTRY — produces the CSV (the 5-min step)
  precompute.py   offline: builds embeddings (no time limit)
  scoring/        hard_filters, features, penalties, behavioral, reasoning
  data/           loader (candidates.jsonl) + embedder (model wrapper)
  utils/          constants (consulting list, company_founded, term sets)
config/         weights.yaml — feature weights & thresholds (no magic numbers)
artifacts/      precomputed embeddings + cached model (runtime needs no network)
data/           candidates.jsonl (gitignored), job_description.txt, sample
sandbox/        Streamlit demo (the required live link)
tests/          pytest — filters, features, output-CSV validation
scripts/        validate_submission.py (organizer's validator)
docs/           plan, rules, architecture diagram
output/         the final <participant_id>.csv
deck/           explainer slides (PDF)
```

---

## Compute constraints (the `rank.py` step)

| Constraint | Limit |
|---|---|
| Runtime | ≤ 5 minutes wall-clock |
| Memory | ≤ 16 GB RAM |
| Compute | CPU only — no GPU |
| Network | OFF — no LLM API calls |
| Disk | ≤ 5 GB |

Precomputation (embeddings) runs offline with no time limit; only the ranking step
that writes the CSV must fit the budget.

---

## Status

🛠️ **Skeleton** — module stubs with docstrings and the intended API. Implement
`src/` bottom-up: `loader` → `hard_filters` → `features` → `rank.py`.

```

# ============================================================
# Redrob Candidate Ranker — dependencies
# Python 3.12.x · CPU-only · no GPU · no network at runtime
# ============================================================

# --- Core numeric / data ---
numpy>=1.26,<2.2
pandas>=2.1,<2.3

# --- Embeddings (CPU) ---
# sentence-transformers pulls in transformers + tokenizers.
# torch CPU wheel keeps it light (no CUDA).
sentence-transformers>=2.7,<3.1
torch>=2.2,<2.6           # CPU build; see README for the --index-url install note
scikit-learn>=1.3,<1.6    # cosine_similarity, helpers, optional LTR baselines

# --- Config / parsing ---
pyyaml>=6.0,<7.0          # config/weights.yaml + submission_metadata.yaml
python-dateutil>=2.8,<3.0 # robust date parsing for last_active / tenure math

# --- Demo / sandbox (required live link) ---
streamlit>=1.31,<1.40

# --- Reading organizer .docx files (offline analysis only) ---
python-docx>=1.1,<2.0

# --- Testing (Stage-4 'real engineering' signal) ---
pytest>=8.0,<9.0

```

# Redrob Hackathon — Submission Metadata Template
#
# Copy this file to your repo root as `submission_metadata.yaml` and fill it in.
# The fields here should match what you submit via the portal at upload time.
# Stage 3 review uses this file to verify your portal metadata.

# ============================================================================
# Team identity
# ============================================================================
team_name: "your-team-name-here"  # Used in leaderboard and announcements

primary_contact:
  name: "Full Name"
  email: "primary@example.com"   # Used for all organizer communication
  phone: "+91-XXXXXXXXXX"        # Used for top-50 / top-10 outreach

team_members:
  - name: "Member 1 Full Name"
    email: "member1@example.com"
    role: "ML Engineer"          # Optional, e.g. "Team Lead", "Backend", "Data"
  - name: "Member 2 Full Name"
    email: "member2@example.com"
    role: "Data Engineer"
  # Add more members as needed; solo participants list just one member

# ============================================================================
# Code and reproducibility
# ============================================================================
github_repo: "https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/YOUR_REPO"
# Required. Must be reachable. Private repos OK if you can grant organizer
# access at Stage 3 (the email to add will be communicated then).

sandbox_link: "https://huggingface.co/spaces/YOUR_USERNAME/redrob-ranker"
# Required. A working hosted environment where the ranker can be run on a small
# candidate sample. See Section 10.5 of submission_spec.md for acceptable
# platforms (HuggingFace Spaces, Streamlit Cloud, Replit, Colab, Docker, Binder).

reproduce_command: "python rank.py --candidates ./candidates.jsonl --out ./submission.csv"
# The single command that produces submission.csv from candidates.jsonl.
# Should run end-to-end within 5 minutes on CPU with 16GB RAM and no network.

# ============================================================================
# Compute environment
# ============================================================================
compute:
  platform: "MacBook Pro M2"                    # Or: "AWS EC2 c5.4xlarge", "Local Linux box", etc.
  cpu_cores: 8                                  # Number of CPU cores used
  ram_gb: 16                                    # Available RAM in GB
  python_version: "3.11.4"                      # python --version
  os: "macOS 14.2"                              # Or "Ubuntu 22.04 LTS", etc.
  uses_gpu_for_inference: false                 # Must be false — see compute constraints
  has_network_during_ranking: false             # Must be false — no API calls during ranking
  pre_computation_required: false               # true if you pre-compute embeddings or train models offline
  pre_computation_time_minutes: 0               # Approximate, if applicable

# ============================================================================
# AI tools declaration
# ============================================================================
# Transparency only — declared use is NOT penalized. Be honest. Stage 5 interview
# may verify these declarations against your code; declarations that contradict
# your code or your interview are flagged.
ai_tools_used:
  - "Claude"        # e.g. for architecture discussion, code review
  - "GitHub Copilot"  # e.g. for autocomplete
  # Other options: "ChatGPT", "Cursor", "Gemini", "Codeium", "Other", "None"

ai_usage_summary: |
  Briefly describe how AI tools were used. Examples:
  - "Used Claude for code review and architectural discussion. Used Copilot for autocomplete.
     No candidate data was fed to any LLM."
  - "Used ChatGPT to debug Python issues and Cursor for refactoring."
  - "No AI tools used."

# ============================================================================
# Approach summary (optional but recommended)
# ============================================================================
methodology_summary: |
  ≤200 word summary of your approach. Strongly recommended.

  Example:
  "Rule-based ranker with explicit reasoning capture. Five scoring components
  (skills, title+career, experience years, location, education) combined with
  a multiplicative behavioral-signal modifier. The title component is the
  decisive signal against keyword-stuffer traps; an endorsement-and-duration
  trust multiplier on skills catches lazy keyword stuffing. Runtime is ~10
  seconds for 50K candidates on CPU."

# ============================================================================
# Declarations
# ============================================================================
declarations:
  read_submission_spec: true        # I have read submission_spec.md in full
  code_is_original_work: true       # My code is my team's original work (using AI as a tool is fine)
  no_collusion: true                # I have not coordinated my submission with other teams
  honeypot_check_done: false        # OPTIONAL — set true if you explicitly checked for honeypots in your ranking
  reproduction_tested: true         # I have tested that my reproduce_command runs end-to-end

```

## File: config\weights.yaml

- Extension: .yaml
- Language: yaml
- Size: 1884 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-23 19:43:41
- Modified: 2026-06-23 19:43:41

### Code

```yaml
# ============================================================
# Feature weights & thresholds — tune here, no magic numbers in code.
# Loaded by src/rank.py. See implementation_plan.md for rationale.
# ============================================================

# --- Feature weights (should sum to ~1.0). Embeddings are ONE feature, not the spine. ---
feature_weights:
  title_role_match:      0.22
  career_evidence:       0.20
  embedding_similarity:  0.18
  experience_fit:        0.12
  product_vs_services:   0.10
  skill_trust:           0.08
  assessment_bonus:      0.05
  writes_code_recently:  0.05

# --- Thresholds used by filters / features / penalties ---
thresholds:
  ideal_exp_min: 5
  ideal_exp_max: 9
  zero_duration_expert_limit: 3        # >=3 expert skills w/ duration 0 => honeypot
  title_chaser_min_jobs: 4
  title_chaser_min_short_tenures: 3
  title_chaser_short_tenure_months: 20
  skill_min_duration_months: 6         # discount skills shorter than this
  langchain_recent_months: 12

# --- JD-specific soft penalties (multipliers applied to feature score) ---
penalties:
  title_chaser:          0.75
  langchain_only:        0.70
  cv_speech_robotics:    0.65
  research_only:         0.60

# --- Behavioral multiplier bounds & knobs ---
behavioral:
  min_multiplier: 0.50
  max_multiplier: 1.10
  response_rate_floor: 0.10            # below this => heavy down-weight
  recency_half_life_months: 9          # decay scale for last_active
  open_to_work_bonus: 0.05
  short_notice_days: 30
  short_notice_bonus: 0.05

# --- Known young companies (start_date before founding => honeypot) ---
# Mirrors src/utils/constants.COMPANY_FOUNDED; keep in sync or load from here.
company_founded:
  krutrim: 2023
  sarvam: 2023
  sarvam ai: 2023

# --- Output ---
output:
  top_k: 100
  score_decimals: 4

```

## File: data\job_description.txt

- Extension: .txt
- Language: plaintext
- Size: 9669 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-23 19:43:41
- Modified: 2026-06-23 19:43:41

### Code

```plaintext
Job Description: Senior AI Engineer — Founding Team
Company: Redrob AI (Series A AI-native talent intelligence platform)
Location: Pune/Noida, India (Hybrid — flexible cadence) | Open to relocation candidates from Tier-1 Indian cities
Employment Type: Full-time
Experience Required: 5–9 years (see "what we mean by this" below)
Let's be honest about this role
We're going to write this JD differently from most. We're a Series A company that just raised our round and we're building a new AI Engineering org from scratch. This is the kind of role where the JD changes every six months because the company changes every six months. So instead of pretending we have a fixed checklist, we're going to tell you what we actually need and what we've gotten wrong before.
If you've spent your career at Google or Meta and you want a well-scoped role with a defined ladder, this isn't it.
If you've spent your career bouncing between early-stage startups and you want to "just code" without having to think about product or recruiter workflows or eval frameworks, this also isn't it.
We need someone who is simultaneously comfortable with two things that sound contradictory:
Deep technical depth in modern ML systems — embeddings, retrieval, ranking, LLMs, fine-tuning.
Scrappy product-engineering attitude — willing to ship a working ranker in a week even if the underlying ML is "obviously suboptimal," because we need to learn from real users before we know what to actually optimize for.
These are not contradictory in real life. They feel contradictory because of how engineering culture sorted itself into "researcher" vs "shipper" archetypes. We need both modes available in the same person, and we'd rather you tilt slightly toward shipper than toward researcher.
What you'd actually be doing
The high-level mandate: own the intelligence layer of Redrob's product. That means the ranking, retrieval, and matching systems that decide what recruiters see when they search for candidates and what candidates see when they search for roles.
In practical terms, your first 90 days will probably look like:
Weeks 1-3: Audit what we currently have (it's mostly BM25 + rule-based scoring, working but not great). Identify the 3-4 highest-leverage things to fix.
Weeks 4-8: Ship a v2 ranking system that demonstrably improves recruiter-engagement metrics. This will involve embeddings, hybrid retrieval, and probably some LLM-based re-ranking, but the architecture is your call.
Weeks 9-12: Set up the evaluation infrastructure — offline benchmarks, online A/B testing, recruiter-feedback loops — so we can keep improving without flying blind.
Beyond that, you'll be driving the long-term architecture of how we do candidate-JD matching at scale, mentoring the next round of hires (we're growing the team from 4 to 12 engineers in the next year), and working closely with our recruiter-experience PM on what to build.
What we mean by "5-9 years"
This is a range, not a requirement. Some people hit "senior engineer" judgment at 4 years; some never hit it after 15. We've used 5-9 because it's roughly where people we've hired into this kind of role have landed, but we'll seriously consider candidates outside the band if other signals are strong.
That said, here are the disqualifiers we actually apply:
If you've spent your career in pure research environments (academic labs, research-only roles) without any production deployment — we will not move forward. We are explicit about this. We've tried it twice and it didn't work for either side.
If your "AI experience" consists primarily of recent (under 12 months) projects using LangChain to call OpenAI — we will probably not move forward, unless you can demonstrate substantial pre-LLM-era ML production experience. We're looking for people who understood retrieval and ranking before it became fashionable.
If you are a senior engineer who hasn't written production code in the last 18 months because you've moved into "architecture" or "tech lead" roles — we will probably not move forward. This role writes code.
The skills inventory (please read carefully)
Most JDs list 20 skills and you're supposed to have all of them. We're going to do this differently.
Things you absolutely need
Production experience with embeddings-based retrieval systems (sentence-transformers, OpenAI embeddings, BGE, E5, or similar) deployed to real users. We don't care which model — we care that you've handled embedding drift, index refresh, retrieval-quality regression in production.
Production experience with vector databases or hybrid search infrastructure — Pinecone, Weaviate, Qdrant, Milvus, OpenSearch, Elasticsearch, FAISS, or something similar. Again, the specific tech doesn't matter; the operational experience does.
Strong Python. Yes really, we care about code quality.
Hands-on experience designing evaluation frameworks for ranking systems — NDCG, MRR, MAP, offline-to-online correlation, A/B test interpretation. If you've never thought about how to evaluate a ranking system rigorously, this role will be very painful.
Things we'd like you to have but won't reject you for
LLM fine-tuning experience (LoRA, QLoRA, PEFT)
Experience with learning-to-rank models (XGBoost-based or neural)
Prior exposure to HR-tech, recruiting tech, or marketplace products
Background in distributed systems or large-scale inference optimization
Open-source contributions in the AI/ML space
Things we explicitly do NOT want
This is the section most JDs skip but we think it's the most important:
Title-chasers. If your career trajectory shows you optimizing for "Senior" → "Staff" → "Principal" titles by switching companies every 1.5 years, we're not a fit. We need someone who plans to be here for 3+ years.
Framework enthusiasts. If your GitHub is full of LangChain tutorials and your blog posts are "How I used [hot framework] to build [demo]" — that's fine but it's not what we need. We need people who think about systems, not frameworks.
People who have only worked at consulting firms (TCS, Infosys, Wipro, Accenture, Cognizant, Capgemini, etc.) in their entire career. We've had bad fit experiences in both directions. If you're currently at one of these companies but have prior product-company experience, that's fine.
People whose primary expertise is computer vision, speech, or robotics without significant NLP/IR exposure. We respect your work but you'd be re-learning fundamentals here.
People whose work has been entirely on closed-source proprietary systems for 5+ years without external validation (papers, talks, open-source). We need to see how you think, not just trust that you can think.
On location, comp, and logistics
Location: Pune/Noida-preferred but flexible. We have offices in Noida and Pune(mostly used Tue/Thu). We don't require any specific number of in-office days but we expect quarterly travel for offsites. Candidates in Hyderabad, Pune, Mumbai, Delhi NCR welcome to apply. Outside India: case-by-case, but we don't sponsor work visas.
Notice period: We'd love sub-30-day notice. We can buy out up to 30 days. 30+ day notice candidates are still in scope but the bar gets higher.
The vibe check
We genuinely believe culture-fit matters more at this stage than skills-fit. Skills are teachable; the rest mostly isn't.
We work async-first and write a lot. If you find writing painful, you'll find this role painful.
We disagree openly and decide quickly. If you find that style abrasive, you'll find this role abrasive.
We move fast and break things, with the caveat that "things" are usually our internal assumptions, not user-facing systems. If you need a stable, mature codebase to be productive, you'll find this role unstable.
How to read between the lines
The "ideal candidate" we're imagining is roughly:
6-8 years total experience, of which 4-5 are in applied ML/AI roles at product companies (not pure services).
Has shipped at least one end-to-end ranking, search, or recommendation system to real users at meaningful scale.
Has strong opinions about retrieval (hybrid vs dense), evaluation (offline vs online), and LLM integration (when to fine-tune vs prompt) — and can defend them with reference to systems they actually built.
Located in or willing to relocate to Noida or Pune.
Active on Redrob platform (or has clear signal of being in the job market) so we can actually talk to them.
We are aware this is a narrow profile. We're not expecting to find many matches in a 100K candidate pool. We're explicitly OK with that — we'd rather see 10 great matches than 1000 maybes.
Final note for the participants of the Redrob hackathon
If you're reading this in the context of the Intelligent Candidate Discovery & Ranking Challenge:
The "right answer" to this JD is not "find candidates whose skills section contains the most AI keywords." That's a trap we've explicitly built into the dataset.
The right answer involves reasoning about the gap between what the JD says and what the JD means. A Tier 5 candidate may not use the words "RAG" or "Pinecone" in their profile, but if their career history shows they built a recommendation system at a product company, they're a fit. A candidate who has all the AI keywords listed as skills but whose title is "Marketing Manager" is not a fit, no matter how perfect their skill list looks.
Your ranking system should also weigh behavioral signals — a perfect-on-paper candidate who hasn't logged in for 6 months and has a 5% recruiter response rate is, for hiring purposes, not actually available. Down-weight them appropriately.
Good luck.
```

```markdown
# data/

Input data for the ranker.

| File | Committed? | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `candidates.jsonl` | ❌ **No** | The 100K pool (~487 MB) — too big for GitHub. Place it here locally, or use Git LFS. The validator/README also accept `candidates.jsonl.gz`. |
| `job_description.txt` | ✅ Yes | The JD as plain text (extracted from the organizer `.docx`). Embedded at precompute time. |
| `sample_candidates.json` | ✅ Yes | First 50 candidates, for the sandbox demo & quick local iteration. |

To get `candidates.jsonl`: copy it from the hackathon bundle, e.g.
```bash
cp "../../[PUB] India_runs_data_and_ai_challenge/India_runs_data_and_ai_challenge/candidates.jsonl" ./data/
```

```

4. Honeypot / buzzword-stuffer / JD-disqualifier handling
5. Reasoning generation & evaluation approach
6. Results / runtime / reproducibility

## File: docs\claude.md

- Extension: .md
- Language: markdown
- Size: 5585 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-23 19:43:41
- Modified: 2026-06-23 19:43:41

### Code

```markdown
# Instructions for Claude — Project Guidelines & Constraints

Strict rules, constraints, and verified data facts that **must be kept in mind at all times** for the Redrob Intelligent Candidate Discovery & Ranking project. Facts below are **verified against the real 100,000-candidate `candidates.jsonl`** and organizer docs. See `implementation_plan.md` for the full plan.

---

## 1. Core Objective
- **Target Role (the ONLY role):** Senior AI Engineer — Founding Team, Redrob AI.
- **Goal:** rank the **top 100** best-fit candidates from **100,000** (`candidates.jsonl`). Output one CSV.
- **Key focus:** real product-engineering experience building/shipping ML/retrieval/ranking systems — NOT buzzword-listers.
- **Strategy:** filter first, rank second. Only ~726 candidates even have an AI/ML title. Use a **transparent weighted feature scorer**; embeddings are ONE feature (~18%), not the spine.

---

## 2. Runtime Constraints (`rank.py` step only)
- Runtime ≤ 5 min wall-clock · Memory ≤ 16 GB · CPU only (no GPU) · Network OFF (no OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini/etc.) · Disk ≤ 5 GB.
- Solution: dual-phase. Precompute embeddings offline (no time limit) → `candidate_embeddings.npy`; load at runtime for instant CPU similarity. Cache model weights in repo so runtime needs no internet.

---

## 3. Honeypot Signatures (auto-DQ risk — >10% in top 100 = disqualified)
Verified counts in real data:
1. **Krutrim** (founded Dec 2023): **38** candidates with start year < 2023 → reject.
2. **Sarvam AI** (founded 2023): **36** candidates with start year < 2023 → reject.
3. **Zero-duration experts:** **21** candidates with ≥3 skills `proficiency=="expert"` AND `duration_months==0` → reject.
   - Use threshold **≥3** zero-duration expert skills (NOT 5; spec example is "10 skills, 0 years").
4. Keep an extensible `company_founded` dict; flag any `start_year < founded_year` for known-young companies.

---

## 4. Anti-Buzzword Stuffer Rules
1. **Embed career DESCRIPTIONS only** — never the skills list. Blob = summary + headline + career-history descriptions.
2. **Title-to-role check:** non-engineering current/primary title (Marketing/HR/Sales/Graphic/Accountant/Civil/Mechanical/etc.) + AI skills → hard filter to 0. (~4,931 such profiles.)
3. **Skill trust:** `duration_months × endorsements`, capped; discount 0-endorsement, <6-month skills.
4. **Assessment cross-ref (BONUS ONLY):** only **24,244 / 100,000** have `skill_assessment_scores`. Use a relevant high score as a positive bonus; **NEVER penalize absence** (most strong candidates lack the relevant one).

---

## 5. JD-Specific Disqualifiers (the JD's "Things we do NOT want")
Apply as soft penalties (not zero):
- **Title-chaser:** ≥4 jobs, ≥3 tenures < 20 months (~5,019). JD wants 3+ year commitment.
- **LangChain-tutorial only:** only recent LangChain/OpenAI work, no pre-2022 real ML.
- **CV/speech/robotics only:** no NLP/retrieval/IR exposure.
- **Research-only:** academic titles, no "production/deployed/shipped/users/scale" in descriptions.
- **Architecture/tech-lead only:** no hands-on code in last 18 months ("this role writes code").

---

## 6. Relocation & Recency (corrected facts)
- **Relocation:** hard-filter only `country != "India"` AND `willing_to_relocate == False` (~17,756). India-based `willing_to_relocate` matters far less. JD welcomes Hyderabad/Pune/Mumbai/Delhi NCR; outside India is "case-by-case", not auto-reject.
- **Recency:** today is **2026**. Last-active 2025 is **NOT** automatically stale (31,472 in 2025, 68,528 in 2026). Use continuous decay by months-since-active, not a 2025 cutoff.

---

## 7. Behavioral Signals (multiplier, ~0.5–1.1)
Multiply feature score by reachability: `recruiter_response_rate` (low = big down-weight), `last_active_date` decay, `open_to_work_flag`, short `notice_period_days`. A 5%-response candidate is effectively unhireable.

---

## 8. CSV Format (any violation = auto-reject; run `validate_submission.py`)
- Filename: `<registered_participant_id>.csv`, UTF-8.
- Columns exact order: `candidate_id,rank,score,reasoning`.
- Exactly **100 data rows** + 1 header.
- `candidate_id` matches `^CAND_[0-9]{7}$` and exists in `candidates.jsonl`.
- Ranks: unique integers 1–100. Scores **non-increasing** by rank. Ties broken by `candidate_id` **ascending**.

---

## 9. Reasoning Column (Stage-4 scored; 10 rows sampled)
1–2 sentences. Must: reference **specific facts** (company + what they built, years, signals); connect to a **JD requirement**; **acknowledge concerns** at lower ranks; vary across candidates; **no hallucination** (no skills/companies not in profile); tone matches rank. The shipped `sample_submission.csv` is a deliberate LOW-quality anti-example — do not imitate its templated style.

---

## 10. Deliverables (all required)
1. `<participant_id>.csv` (validated). 2. GitHub repo + `requirements.txt` + README single-command reproduce; **commit incrementally** (git history is reviewed). 3. `submission_metadata.yaml` at root (honest AI-tools declaration — declaring Claude is NOT penalized). 4. **Live sandbox/demo link** (HuggingFace Space / Streamlit Cloud, CPU-only, no network, ranks ≤100 sample) — REQUIRED. 5. Explainer PDF.

---

## 11. Scoring (what we optimize for)
`Final = 0.50×NDCG@10 + 0.30×NDCG@50 + 0.15×MAP + 0.05×P@10`. **80% of the grade is the top 50** — getting the ordering of the best ~150 right matters most. Max 3 submissions; last valid counts; no live leaderboard.

```

## File: docs\implementation_plan.md

- Extension: .md
- Language: markdown
- Size: 12128 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-23 19:43:41
- Modified: 2026-06-23 19:43:41

### Code

```markdown
# Intelligent Candidate Discovery & Ranking — Implementation Plan

Rank the **top 100** best-fit candidates out of **100,000** for ONE role: **Senior AI Engineer — Founding Team** at Redrob AI. Output a CSV. That's the whole task.

This plan reflects facts **verified against the real 100,000-candidate `candidates.jsonl`** and against all organizer docs (JD, submission spec, signals doc, schema).

---

## 1. The Core Strategy (one line)

Only ~726 of 100,000 candidates even have an AI/ML title. So this is a **filtering problem first, ranking problem second**: throw out the obvious junk with cheap rules, then carefully score the few hundred real candidates with a **transparent weighted feature model** where embedding similarity is just ONE feature (~18%), not the backbone.

### Why embeddings are NOT the spine
- **80% of the grade is the top 50** (`0.50×NDCG@10 + 0.30×NDCG@50 + 0.15×MAP + 0.05×P@10`). Getting the ordering of the best ~150 people right is everything.
- A 384-dim MiniLM cosine score **cannot reliably separate the #3 candidate from the #15** — and that's exactly where the points live.
- The dataset contains "behavioral twins": profiles stuffed with the right keywords whose own descriptions admit weakness ("production was handled by the platform team", "lighter weight than ranking systems at FAANG"). Embeddings love them; a feature scorer that reads the JD's real disqualifiers rejects them.
- Everything must be **explainable** for the Stage-5 defend-your-work interview. "The feature weights ranked them" is defensible; "the cosine put them there" is not.

---

## 2. Verified Dataset Facts (from the real 100K file)

| Finding | Count | Use |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Candidates with an ML/AI/DS-ish title | **726 / 100,000** | The "needles" are <1% — filter aggressively |
| Honeypot: Krutrim start < 2023 (founded Dec 2023) | **38** | Hard filter → score 0 |
| Honeypot: Sarvam start < 2023 (founded 2023) | **36** | Hard filter → score 0 |
| Honeypot: ≥3 skills "expert" with `duration_months == 0` | **21** | Hard filter → score 0 |
| Consulting-only careers (TCS/Infosys/Wipro/etc.) | **9,745** | Hard filter → score 0 |
| Foreign (`country != India`) AND `willing_to_relocate == False` | **17,756** | Hard filter → score 0 |
| Non-eng title (Marketing/HR/Sales/etc.) + AI skills (stuffers) | **4,931** | Hard filter → score 0 |
| Title-chasers (≥4 jobs, ≥3 tenures < 20 months) | **5,019** | Penalty (not zero) |
| Have any `skill_assessment_scores` | **24,244** (only 24%) | Bonus when present — **never penalize absence** |
| `last_active_date` in 2025 / 2026 | **31,472 / 68,528** | Recency = continuous decay, **NOT** "2025 = dead" |

> Today is **2026**. Do not treat a 2025 last-active date as automatically stale.

---

## 3. The Target Profile (what "Senior AI Engineer" means HERE)

The JD is explicit. The right answer is matching the *meaning*, not the title/keywords.

**Must have:** production embeddings-based retrieval, vector DB / hybrid search, strong Python, ranking evaluation (NDCG/MRR/MAP/A-B).
**Ideal:** 6–8 yrs total, 4–5 in applied ML at **product** companies, shipped a ranking/search/recommendation system, in or willing to relocate to **Noida/Pune**, **active** (reachable).
**Explicitly NOT wanted:** title-chasers · LangChain-tutorial/framework enthusiasts · consulting-only · CV/speech/robotics without NLP/IR · research-only without production · architecture/tech-lead who hasn't coded in 18 months.

---

## 4. Hackathon Rules & Constraints

### Runtime constraints (the `rank.py` step only)
- **Runtime:** ≤ 5 minutes wall-clock
- **Memory:** ≤ 16 GB RAM
- **Compute:** CPU only — no GPU
- **Network:** OFF — no API calls (OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini/etc.). Fully self-contained.
- **Disk:** ≤ 5 GB intermediate state
- Precomputation (embeddings) is allowed offline with **no** time limit; only `rank.py` must fit the budget.

### CSV format (auto-rejected on any violation — run `validate_submission.py`)
- Filename: `<registered_participant_id>.csv`, UTF-8
- Columns in exact order: `candidate_id,rank,score,reasoning`
- Exactly **100 data rows** + 1 header
- `candidate_id` matches `^CAND_[0-9]{7}$` and exists in `candidates.jsonl`
- Ranks: unique integers 1–100; scores **non-increasing** by rank; ties broken by `candidate_id` **ascending**

### Honeypot rule
Submissions with **>10% honeypots in the top 100 are disqualified** at Stage 3.

---

## 5. Architecture — Two Phases

### Phase 1 — Offline Precompute (your PC, no time limit)
1. Read `candidates.jsonl` (100K).
2. Per candidate, build text blob = `summary + headline + all career-history descriptions`. **NO skills list** (that's how stuffers game it).
3. Encode with local `all-MiniLM-L6-v2` (384-dim).
4. Save `candidate_embeddings.npy` (~150 MB).
5. Embed the JD once → `jd_embedding.npy`.
6. Cache model weights into the repo (so runtime needs no internet). Commit artifacts.

### Phase 2 — `rank.py` (≤5 min, CPU, no network)
1. Load `candidates.jsonl` + `candidate_embeddings.npy` + `jd_embedding.npy`.
2. **Hard filters** → score 0 (see §6).
3. **Feature score** for survivors (see §7).
4. **JD penalties** (see §8).
5. **Behavioral multiplier** (see §9).
6. Sort desc; ties by `candidate_id` ascending; take top 100.
7. Generate **specific, varied, honest** reasoning (see §10).
8. Write `<participant_id>.csv`; run `validate_submission.py`.

---

## 6. Hard Filters (cheap rules, score → 0)

- **Honeypot — company founding:** keep `company_founded = {"krutrim":2023, "sarvam":2023, ...}`; flag any `start_year < founded_year`.
- **Honeypot — fake experts:** ≥3 skills `proficiency == "expert"` AND `duration_months == 0`.
- **Consulting-only:** every company in career history ∈ consulting set.
- **Can't relocate:** `country != "India"` AND `willing_to_relocate == False`.
- **Buzzword stuffer:** title clearly non-engineering (Marketing/HR/Sales/Graphic/Accountant/Civil/Mechanical…) AND claims core AI skills.

---

## 7. Feature Score (survivors only, 0–1)

Embedding similarity is ONE input, not the boss.

| Feature | Measures | Weight |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `title_role_match` | Current/recent title is really ML/AI/DS/eng | **0.22** |
| `career_evidence` | Descriptions mention retrieval/ranking/search/recommendation/NLP | **0.20** |
| `embedding_similarity` | cosine(JD, career text) — coarse semantic match | **0.18** |
| `experience_fit` | 5–9 yrs ideal; soft penalty outside (JD says it's a range) | **0.12** |
| `product_vs_services` | Bonus product companies; penalty services | **0.10** |
| `skill_trust` | `duration_months × endorsements`, capped | **0.08** |
| `assessment_bonus` | Relevant Redrob assessment present → bonus (never penalize absence) | **0.05** |
| `writes_code_recently` | Penalize architecture/tech-lead-only with no recent code | **0.05** |

---

## 8. JD-Specific Penalties (free points)

Soft penalties (reduce score, don't zero out):
- **Title-chaser:** ≥4 jobs with ≥3 tenures < 20 months.
- **LangChain-only:** only recent LangChain/OpenAI work, no pre-2022 real ML.
- **CV/speech/robotics-only:** no NLP/retrieval exposure.
- **Research-only:** academic titles, no "production/deployed/shipped/users/scale" in descriptions.

---

## 9. Behavioral Multiplier (~0.5–1.1)

Multiply feature score by "can we actually hire them":
- `recruiter_response_rate` — low = unreachable = big down-weight.
- `last_active_date` — decay by **months since active** (today 2026), not a 2025 cutoff.
- `open_to_work_flag`, short `notice_period_days` — small bonuses.

Multiplier, not a filter: a great-but-slightly-less-active candidate shouldn't drop to zero, but a 5%-response-rate candidate effectively does.

---

## 10. Reasoning Column (Stage-4 make-or-break)

Spec penalizes templated/name-insert reasoning; samples 10 rows for variety, specificity, honest concerns, tone-matches-rank. Each reasoning must:
1. Name **specific evidence** (company + what they built).
2. Connect to a **JD requirement**.
3. For lower ranks, **name the concern** honestly ("strong retrieval background but 120-day notice").
Vary sentence structure. Never invent a skill/company not in the profile.

---

## 11. Master Flowchart

```mermaid

## File: scripts\validate_submission.py

- Extension: .py
- Language: python
- Size: 5201 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-23 19:43:41
- Modified: 2026-06-23 19:43:41

### Code

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Validate submission CSV per challenge rules (sections 2–3).
Row 1 = header. Rows 2–101 = exactly 100 data rows. CSV only.
"""

import csv
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REQUIRED_HEADER = ["candidate_id", "rank", "score", "reasoning"]
CANDIDATE_ID_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^CAND_[0-9]{7}$")
DATA_ROW_START = 2
EXPECTED_DATA_ROWS = 100


def validate_submission(csv_path):
    errors = []
    path = Path(csv_path)

    if path.suffix.lower() != ".csv":
        errors.append("Filename must use a .csv extension.")
    elif not path.stem:
        errors.append("Filename must be your registered participant ID (e.g. team_xxx.csv).")

    try:
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as f:
            reader = csv.reader(f)

            try:
                header = next(reader)
            except StopIteration:
                errors.append("Row 1 must be the header row; file is empty.")
                return errors

            # Row 1: column names and their order come from this line only
            if header != REQUIRED_HEADER:
                errors.append(
                    "Row 1 (header) must be exactly:\n"
                    f"  {','.join(REQUIRED_HEADER)}\n"
                    f"Found:\n"
                    f"  {','.join(header)}"
                )

            data_rows = []
            for row in reader:
                if any(cell.strip() for cell in row):
                    data_rows.append(row)

    except UnicodeDecodeError:
        errors.append("File must be UTF-8 encoded.")
        return errors
    except OSError as e:
        errors.append(f"Cannot read file: {e}")
        return errors

    n = len(data_rows)
    if n != EXPECTED_DATA_ROWS:
        errors.append(
            f"After the header (row 1), there must be exactly {EXPECTED_DATA_ROWS} "
            f"data rows (rows {DATA_ROW_START}–{DATA_ROW_START + EXPECTED_DATA_ROWS - 1}); "
            f"found {n}."
        )

    seen_ids = set()
    seen_ranks = set()
    by_rank = []

    for i, cells in enumerate(data_rows):
        row_num = DATA_ROW_START + i

        if len(cells) != len(REQUIRED_HEADER):
            errors.append(
                f"Row {row_num}: expected {len(REQUIRED_HEADER)} columns "
                f"({','.join(REQUIRED_HEADER)}), got {len(cells)}."
            )
            continue

        row = dict(zip(REQUIRED_HEADER, cells))
        cid = row["candidate_id"].strip()
        rank_s = row["rank"].strip()
        score_s = row["score"].strip()

        if not cid:
            errors.append(f"Row {row_num}: candidate_id is required.")
        elif not CANDIDATE_ID_PATTERN.match(cid):
            errors.append(
                f"Row {row_num}: candidate_id must be CAND_XXXXXXX (7 digits)."
            )
        elif cid in seen_ids:
            errors.append(f"Row {row_num}: duplicate candidate_id '{cid}'.")
        else:
            seen_ids.add(cid)

        try:
            rank = int(rank_s)
            if str(rank) != rank_s:
                raise ValueError
            if not 1 <= rank <= 100:
                errors.append(f"Row {row_num}: rank must be between 1 and 100.")
            elif rank in seen_ranks:
                errors.append(f"Row {row_num}: duplicate rank {rank}.")
            else:
                seen_ranks.add(rank)
        except ValueError:
            errors.append(f"Row {row_num}: rank must be an integer (1–100).")
            rank = None

        try:
            score = float(score_s)
        except ValueError:
            errors.append(f"Row {row_num}: score must be a float.")
            score = None

        if rank is not None and score is not None and cid:
            by_rank.append((rank, score, cid))

    missing = set(range(1, 101)) - seen_ranks
    if missing:
        errors.append(
            f"Each rank 1–100 must appear exactly once; missing: {sorted(missing)}"
        )

    by_rank.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])

    for i in range(len(by_rank) - 1):
        r1, s1, _ = by_rank[i]
        r2, s2, _ = by_rank[i + 1]
        if s1 < s2:
            errors.append(
                f"score must be non-increasing by rank: "
                f"rank {r1} ({s1}) < rank {r2} ({s2})."
            )

    for i in range(len(by_rank) - 1):
        r1, s1, c1 = by_rank[i]
        r2, s2, c2 = by_rank[i + 1]
        if s1 == s2 and c1 > c2:
            errors.append(
                f"Equal scores at ranks {r1} and {r2}: "
                f"tie-break requires candidate_id ascending "
                f"({c1!r} > {c2!r})."
            )

    return errors


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 2:
        print("Usage: python validate_submission.py <participant_id>.csv")
        sys.exit(1)

    errors = validate_submission(sys.argv[1])
    if errors:
        print(f"Validation failed ({len(errors)} issue(s)):\n")
        for e in errors:
            print(f"- {e}")
        sys.exit(1)

    print("Submission is valid.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

## File: src\precompute.py

- Extension: .py
- Language: python
- Size: 2592 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-26 19:13:40
- Modified: 2026-06-26 19:47:01

### Code

```python
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
from tqdm import tqdm

from src.data.loader import (
    load_candidates,
    build_text_blob
)

from src.data.embedder import (
    cache_model,
    load_model,
    embed_texts
)


def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:

    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Precompute candidate + JD embeddings."
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--candidates",
        required=True
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--jd",
        default="data/job_description.txt"
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--out-dir",
        default="artifacts"
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--cache-model",
        action="store_true"
    )

    return parser.parse_args()


def main() -> None:

    args = parse_args()

    out_dir = Path(args.out_dir)

    out_dir.mkdir(
        parents=True,
        exist_ok=True
    )

    if args.cache_model:

        print("[precompute] caching model...")
        cache_model()

    print("[precompute] loading model...")
    model = load_model()

    candidate_ids = []
    texts = []

    print("[precompute] reading candidates...")

    for candidate in tqdm(
            load_candidates(args.candidates)):

        candidate_ids.append(
            candidate["candidate_id"]
        )

        texts.append(
            build_text_blob(candidate)
        )

    print(
        f"[precompute] embedding {len(texts):,} candidates..."
    )

    candidate_embeddings = embed_texts(
        model=model,
        texts=texts,
        batch_size=256
    )

    candidate_embeddings = candidate_embeddings.astype(
        np.float32,
        copy=False
    )

    np.save(
        out_dir / "candidate_embeddings.npy",
        candidate_embeddings
    )

    np.save(
        out_dir / "candidate_ids.npy",
        np.array(candidate_ids)
    )

    print("[precompute] embedding JD...")

    with open(
            args.jd,
            "r",
            encoding="utf-8"
    ) as f:

        jd_text = f.read()

    jd_embedding = embed_texts(
        model=model,
        texts=[jd_text]
    )

    jd_embedding = jd_embedding.astype(
        np.float32,
        copy=False
    )

    np.save(
        out_dir / "jd_embedding.npy",
        jd_embedding
    )

    print("[precompute] done.")

    print(
        f"[precompute] saved to {out_dir}"
    )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
"""
rank.py — MAIN ENTRY POINT (the graded ≤5-minute step).

Reproduce command (goes in README + submission_metadata.yaml):
    python src/rank.py --candidates ./data/candidates.jsonl --out ./output/<participant_id>.csv

Constraints this file MUST honor:
    - Runtime  : <= 5 minutes wall-clock
    - Memory   : <= 16 GB RAM
    - Compute  : CPU only (no GPU)
    - Network  : OFF (no API calls) — embeddings are precomputed offline
    - Disk     : <= 5 GB

Pipeline (see implementation_plan.md):
    1. Load candidates.jsonl + artifacts/candidate_embeddings.npy + artifacts/jd_embedding.npy
    2. HARD FILTER   -> score 0   (scoring/hard_filters.py)
    3. FEATURE SCORE -> 0..1      (scoring/features.py)
    4. JD PENALTIES  -> reduce    (scoring/penalties.py)
    5. BEHAVIORAL    -> x multiplier (scoring/behavioral.py)
    6. SORT desc; ties by candidate_id ascending; take top 100
    7. REASONING                  (scoring/reasoning.py)
    8. WRITE <participant_id>.csv ; then run scripts/validate_submission.py

TODO: implement. This is a placeholder skeleton.
"""

"""
Tests for src/scoring/hard_filters.py.

Each test feeds a minimal candidate dict and asserts the filter fires correctly.
Run: python -m pytest tests/test_hard_filters.py -v
"""

import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Make the repo root importable when pytest runs from anywhere.
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))

from src.scoring.hard_filters import (  # noqa: E402
    is_honeypot_company_founding,
    is_honeypot_zero_duration_expert,
    is_honeypot_experience_inflation,
    is_consulting_only,
    cannot_relocate,
    is_buzzword_stuffer,
    is_disqualified,
)
from src.utils.constants import (  # noqa: E402
    CONSULTING_FIRMS,
    COMPANY_FOUNDED,
    NON_ENG_TITLES,
    AI_SKILL_TERMS,
)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Helpers
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def cand(profile=None, career=None, skills=None, signals=None):
    return {
        "candidate_id": "CAND_0000001",
        "profile": profile or {},
        "career_history": career or [],
        "skills": skills or [],
        "redrob_signals": signals or {},
    }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 1. Honeypot — company founding
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_honeypot_krutrim_before_2023():
    c = cand(career=[{"company": "Krutrim", "start_date": "2020-01-01"}])
    assert is_honeypot_company_founding(c, COMPANY_FOUNDED) is True


def test_honeypot_company_founding_ok_after_founding():
    c = cand(career=[{"company": "Krutrim", "start_date": "2024-01-01"}])
    assert is_honeypot_company_founding(c, COMPANY_FOUNDED) is False


def test_honeypot_company_founding_unknown_company_ignored():
    c = cand(career=[{"company": "Swiggy", "start_date": "2015-01-01"}])
    assert is_honeypot_company_founding(c, COMPANY_FOUNDED) is False


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 2. Honeypot — zero-duration expert (threshold = 3)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_zero_duration_expert_three_triggers():
    skills = [{"name": s, "proficiency": "expert", "duration_months": 0} for s in ("a", "b", "c")]
    assert is_honeypot_zero_duration_expert(cand(skills=skills)) is True


def test_zero_duration_expert_two_does_not_trigger():
    skills = [{"name": s, "proficiency": "expert", "duration_months": 0} for s in ("a", "b")]
    assert is_honeypot_zero_duration_expert(cand(skills=skills)) is False


def test_zero_duration_expert_with_duration_ignored():
    # expert but with real duration -> not a honeypot signal
    skills = [{"name": s, "proficiency": "expert", "duration_months": 24} for s in ("a", "b", "c")]
    assert is_honeypot_zero_duration_expert(cand(skills=skills)) is False


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 3. Honeypot — experience inflation
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_experience_inflation_triggers():
    # claims 14 yrs, career history sums to ~1 yr (12 months)
    c = cand(
        profile={"years_of_experience": 14.0},
        career=[{"company": "X", "duration_months": 12}],
    )
    assert is_honeypot_experience_inflation(c) is True


def test_experience_inflation_consistent_ok():
    # claims 7 yrs, career sums to ~7 yrs (84 months) -> fine
    c = cand(
        profile={"years_of_experience": 7.0},
        career=[{"company": "X", "duration_months": 84}],
    )
    assert is_honeypot_experience_inflation(c) is False


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 4. Consulting-only
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_consulting_only_all_consulting():
    c = cand(career=[{"company": "TCS"}, {"company": "Infosys"}])
    assert is_consulting_only(c, CONSULTING_FIRMS) is True


def test_consulting_only_mixed_kept():
    # has product-company experience too -> not consulting-only
    c = cand(career=[{"company": "TCS"}, {"company": "Swiggy"}])
    assert is_consulting_only(c, CONSULTING_FIRMS) is False


def test_consulting_only_empty_history_kept():
    assert is_consulting_only(cand(career=[]), CONSULTING_FIRMS) is False


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 5. Cannot relocate
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_cannot_relocate_foreign_unwilling():
    c = cand(profile={"country": "USA"}, signals={"willing_to_relocate": False})
    assert cannot_relocate(c) is True


def test_relocate_foreign_but_willing_kept():
    c = cand(profile={"country": "USA"}, signals={"willing_to_relocate": True})
    assert cannot_relocate(c) is False


def test_relocate_india_unwilling_kept():
    # India-based + won't relocate must be KEPT (may already be in-region)
    c = cand(profile={"country": "India"}, signals={"willing_to_relocate": False})
    assert cannot_relocate(c) is False


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 6. Buzzword stuffer (substring title + AI skill + no real work)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_buzzword_stuffer_triggers():
    c = cand(
        profile={"current_title": "Marketing Manager", "summary": "ran ad campaigns"},
        career=[{"description": "managed marketing budgets and brand campaigns"}],
        skills=[{"name": "PyTorch"}, {"name": "LLM"}],
    )
    assert is_buzzword_stuffer(c, NON_ENG_TITLES, AI_SKILL_TERMS) is True


def test_buzzword_stuffer_substring_title():
    # "Senior Marketing Manager" should still match via substring
    c = cand(
        profile={"current_title": "Senior Marketing Manager", "summary": "campaigns"},
        career=[{"description": "brand strategy"}],
        skills=[{"name": "NLP"}],
    )
    assert is_buzzword_stuffer(c, NON_ENG_TITLES, AI_SKILL_TERMS) is True


def test_buzzword_stuffer_spared_if_real_ai_job_title():
    # non-eng CURRENT title + AI skill BUT previously held a real AI job title -> spared
    c = cand(
        profile={"current_title": "Marketing Manager", "summary": "courses on rag"},
        career=[
            {"title": "ML Engineer", "description": "built a production ranking system"},
            {"title": "Marketing Manager", "description": "campaigns"},
        ],
        skills=[{"name": "PyTorch"}],
    )
    assert is_buzzword_stuffer(c, NON_ENG_TITLES, AI_SKILL_TERMS) is False


def test_buzzword_stuffer_not_spared_by_seeded_summary():
    # the dataset's trap: AI buzzwords seeded in summary/description but NO real AI job title
    c = cand(
        profile={
            "current_title": "Project Manager",
            "summary": "took online courses on rag and vector databases, langchain side projects",
        },
        career=[{"title": "Project Manager", "description": "led project delivery; rag experiments"}],
        skills=[{"name": "Vector Database"}, {"name": "LLM"}],
    )
    assert is_buzzword_stuffer(c, NON_ENG_TITLES, AI_SKILL_TERMS) is True


def test_buzzword_stuffer_eng_title_not_flagged():
    c = cand(
        profile={"current_title": "ML Engineer"},
        skills=[{"name": "PyTorch"}],
    )
    assert is_buzzword_stuffer(c, NON_ENG_TITLES, AI_SKILL_TERMS) is False


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 7. Master gate + robustness
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def test_is_disqualified_clean_candidate():
    c = cand(
        profile={"country": "India", "current_title": "ML Engineer", "years_of_experience": 6.0},
        career=[{"company": "Swiggy", "start_date": "2019-01-01", "duration_months": 60}],
        skills=[{"name": "PyTorch", "proficiency": "advanced", "duration_months": 36}],
        signals={"willing_to_relocate": True},
    )
    dq, reason = is_disqualified(c)
    assert dq is False and reason == ""


def test_is_disqualified_returns_reason():
    c = cand(career=[{"company": "Sarvam AI", "start_date": "2019-01-01"}])
    dq, reason = is_disqualified(c)
    assert dq is True and reason == "Impossible company joining date"


def test_robustness_empty_dict():
    # must not crash on a completely empty candidate
    dq, reason = is_disqualified({})
    assert dq is False


def test_robustness_none_fields():
    # fields present but explicitly None -> must not crash
    c = {
        "profile": None,
        "career_history": None,
        "skills": None,
        "redrob_signals": None,
    }
    dq, reason = is_disqualified(c)
    assert dq is False


def test_robustness_none_inner_values():
    c = {
        "profile": {"current_title": None, "country": None, "years_of_experience": None},
        "career_history": [{"company": None, "start_date": None, "duration_months": None}],
        "skills": [{"name": None, "proficiency": None, "duration_months": None}],
        "redrob_signals": {"willing_to_relocate": None},
    }
    dq, reason = is_disqualified(c)
    assert dq is False

## File: src\data\embedder.py

- Size: 3230 bytes

```python
"""
embedder.py — wraps the local sentence-transformer model.

The model weights are CACHED INTO THE REPO (artifacts/model/) so rank.py can run
with the NETWORK OFF. We never hit huggingface.co at runtime.

Usage:
    # one-time, online, to cache the model into the repo:
    python -m src.data.embedder --cache

    # in code (offline-safe):
    from src.data.embedder import load_model, embed_texts
    model = load_model()
    vecs = embed_texts(model, ["some text", ...])
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import os
from pathlib import Path

# Small, fast on CPU, 384-dim. The plan standardized on this.
MODEL_NAME = "sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2"

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
MODEL_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "artifacts" / "model"


def cache_model() -> None:
    """Download the model ONCE (online) and save it into artifacts/model/."""
    from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

    print(f"[embedder] downloading {MODEL_NAME} ...")
    model = SentenceTransformer(MODEL_NAME)
    MODEL_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    model.save(str(MODEL_DIR))
    print(f"[embedder] cached to {MODEL_DIR}")


def load_model():
    """
    Load model from local cache.

    Offline-safe during rank.py execution.
    Falls back to hub only if cache is missing.
    """

    from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

    if MODEL_DIR.exists() and any(MODEL_DIR.iterdir()):

        os.environ["HF_HUB_OFFLINE"] = "1"
        os.environ["TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE"] = "1"

        return SentenceTransformer(
            str(MODEL_DIR),
            device="cpu"
        )

    print(
        f"[embedder] WARNING: local cache {MODEL_DIR} missing."
    )

    return SentenceTransformer(
        MODEL_NAME,
        device="cpu"
    )


def embed_texts(
    model,
    texts,
    batch_size: int = 256
):
    """
    Encode strings into L2-normalized float32 vectors.

    Returns
    -------
    np.ndarray
        Shape (N,384), dtype float32.
    """

    import numpy as np
    import torch

    if len(texts) == 0:

        dim = model.get_sentence_embedding_dimension()

        return np.empty(
            (0, dim),
            dtype=np.float32
        )

    with torch.inference_mode():

        embeddings = model.encode(
            texts,
            batch_size=batch_size,
            show_progress_bar=True,
            convert_to_numpy=True,
            normalize_embeddings=True
        )

    return embeddings.astype(
        np.float32,
        copy=False
    )

if __name__ == "__main__":

    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()

    parser.add_argument(
        "--cache",
        action="store_true"
    )

    args = parser.parse_args()

    if args.cache:

        cache_model()

    else:

        model = load_model()

        vec = embed_texts(
            model,
            ["hello world"]
        )

        print(
            f"[embedder] loaded OK."
        )

        print(
            f"dim = {vec.shape[1]}"
        )

        print(
            f"dtype = {vec.dtype}"
        )
```

## File: src\data\loader.py

- Modified: 2026-06-26 19:14:00

### Code

```python
"""
loader.py — stream and parse candidates.jsonl safely.

Handles both plain .jsonl and gzipped .jsonl.gz (the validator/README mention both).
Streams line-by-line so we never load all 100K dicts at once unnecessarily.

API:
    load_candidates(path) -> Iterator[dict]   # streams one candidate dict per line
    build_text_blob(candidate) -> str         # profile.summary + profile.headline +
                                              # career-history descriptions
                                              # (NO skills list — anti-stuffer rule)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import gzip
import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Iterator


def _s(value) -> str:
    """Coerce any value to a clean string (None -> '')."""
    if value is None:
        return ""
    return str(value).strip()


def load_candidates(path) -> Iterator[dict]:
    """Yield one candidate dict per line from .jsonl or .jsonl.gz."""
    path = Path(path)
    opener = gzip.open if path.suffix == ".gz" else open

    with opener(path, "rt", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for line in f:
            line = line.strip()
            if not line:
                continue
            try:
                yield json.loads(line)
            except json.JSONDecodeError:
                # Skip a malformed line rather than crash the whole run.
                continue


def build_text_blob(candidate: dict) -> str:
    """
    Build the text we embed.

    Includes:
        - profile.headline
        - profile.summary
        - each career_history entry's title + description

    Intentionally EXCLUDES the skills list, so keyword stuffers who paste
    "PyTorch, LLM, RAG" into skills cannot game the embedding. The embedding
    reflects what the candidate actually DID (their story + work), not what
    they clicked.
    """
    candidate = candidate if isinstance(candidate, dict) else {}
    profile = candidate.get("profile") or {}

    chunks = []

    # NOTE: headline & summary live under "profile", not at the top level.
    headline = _s(profile.get("headline"))
    summary = _s(profile.get("summary"))
    if headline:
        chunks.append(headline)
    if summary:
        chunks.append(summary)

    career = candidate.get("career_history") or []
    for exp in career:
        exp = exp if isinstance(exp, dict) else {}
        title = _s(exp.get("title"))
        description = _s(exp.get("description"))
        if title:
            chunks.append(title)
        if description:
            chunks.append(description)

    return "\n".join(chunks)

```

## File: src\scoring\features.py

```python
"""
features.py — weighted feature scoring.

Each feature returns a score in [0,1].

Embedding similarity is ONLY one feature (~18%), not the backbone.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

from src.utils.constants import (
    ENG_TITLE_TERMS,
    RETRIEVAL_TERMS,
    CONSULTING_FIRMS,
    PRODUCT_COMPANIES,
)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def _safe_lower(value) -> str:
    """Safely convert any value to lowercase string."""
    if value is None:
        return ""
    return str(value).strip().lower()


def _career_history(candidate: dict) -> list:
    return candidate.get("career_history", [])


def _candidate_title(candidate: dict) -> str:
    return _safe_lower(
        candidate.get("current_title")
        or candidate.get("headline")
        or ""
    )


def _experience_years(candidate: dict) -> float:
    """
    Try several possible keys because datasets differ.
    """

    possible = [
        "years_experience",
        "experience_years",
        "total_experience_years",
    ]

    for key in possible:
        value = candidate.get(key)

        if value is not None:
            try:
                return float(value)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # fallback:
    total_months = candidate.get("total_experience_months")

    if total_months is not None:
        return float(total_months) / 12.0

    return 0.0


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Feature 1
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def title_role_match(candidate: dict) -> float:
    """
    Measures how well the current title matches
    Senior AI Engineer.
    """

    title = _candidate_title(candidate)

    strong_matches = {
        "ai engineer",
        "machine learning engineer",
        "ml engineer",
        "retrieval engineer",
        "search engineer",
        "nlp engineer",
        "recommendation engineer",
    }

    medium_matches = {
        "data scientist",
        "applied scientist",
        "research engineer",
        "software engineer",
        "backend engineer",
    }

    if any(term in title for term in strong_matches):
        return 1.0

    if any(term in title for term in medium_matches):
        return 0.75

    if any(term in title for term in ENG_TITLE_TERMS):
        return 0.6

    return 0.0


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Feature 2
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def career_evidence(
    candidate: dict,
    retrieval_terms: set = RETRIEVAL_TERMS,
) -> float:
    """
    Looks for retrieval/ranking evidence inside
    career descriptions.
    """

    corpus = []

    for exp in _career_history(candidate):

        corpus.append(
            _safe_lower(
                exp.get("description", "")
            )
        )

    text = " ".join(corpus)

    if not text:
        return 0.0

    hits = sum(
        term in text
        for term in retrieval_terms
    )

    return min(
        hits / 6.0,
        1.0,
    )


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Feature 3
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def experience_fit(
    candidate: dict,
    ideal_min: float = 5,
    ideal_max: float = 9,
) -> float:
    """
    Smooth score.

    Ideal = 5–9 years.
    """

    years = _experience_years(candidate)

    if ideal_min <= years <= ideal_max:
        return 1.0

    distance = min(
        abs(years - ideal_min),
        abs(years - ideal_max),
    )

    score = 1.0 - (distance * 0.1)

    return max(
        0.2,
        min(score, 1.0),
    )


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Feature 4
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def product_vs_services(
    candidate: dict,
    consulting_firms: set = CONSULTING_FIRMS,
    product_companies: set = PRODUCT_COMPANIES,
) -> float:
    """
    Product companies are preferred.

    Consulting-heavy careers receive lower scores.
    """

    score = 0

    for exp in _career_history(candidate):

        company = _safe_lower(
            exp.get("company", "")
        )

        if company in product_companies:
            score += 1

        elif company in consulting_firms:
            score -= 1

    score = (score + 3) / 6

    return max(
        0.0,
        min(score, 1.0),
    )
    
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Feature 5
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

def skill_trust(
    candidate: Dict,
    cap: float = 1.0,
) -> float:
    """
    Estimate how trustworthy the declared skills are.

    High score:
        • Long usage duration
        • Endorsements
        • Multiple mature skills

    Low score:
        • 0 endorsements
        • Very short duration
        • Freshly added buzzwords
    """

    skills = _skills(candidate)

    if not skills:
        return 0.0

    weighted_sum = 0.0
    valid_skills = 0

    for skill in skills:

        duration = skill.get("duration_months", 0)
        endorsements = skill.get("endorsements", 0)

        try:
            duration = float(duration)
        except Exception:
            duration = 0.0

        try:
            endorsements = float(endorsements)
        except Exception:
            endorsements = 0.0

        # Ignore almost-unused skills
        if duration < 3:
            continue

        score = duration

        # Endorsements increase confidence
        score *= (1.0 + min(endorsements, 10) / 10.0)

        # Discount weak evidence
        if endorsements == 0:
            score *= 0.60

        # Very new skills receive less trust
        if duration < 6:
            score *= 0.75

        weighted_sum += score
        valid_skills += 1

    if valid_skills == 0:
        return 0.0

    average = weighted_sum / valid_skills

    # Normalize approximately to [0,1]
    normalized = average / 36.0

    return min(normalized, cap)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Internal helper
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

def _assessment_score(candidate: Dict) -> float:
    """
    Returns the highest assessment score if available.

    Handles:
        None
        []
        {}
        mixed formats
    """

    signals = _signals(candidate)

    assessments = signals.get(
        "skill_assessment_scores",
        None,
    )

    if not assessments:
        return 0.0

    values = []

    if isinstance(assessments, dict):

        for value in assessments.values():

            try:
                values.append(float(value))
            except Exception:
                pass

    elif isinstance(assessments, list):

        for item in assessments:

            if isinstance(item, dict):

                score = item.get("score")

                try:
                    values.append(float(score))
                except Exception:
                    pass

    if not values:
        return 0.0

    return max(values)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Feature 6
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

def assessment_bonus(
    candidate: Dict,
    relevant_skills: set,
) -> float:
    """
    Bonus only.

    Never penalize candidates
    who have no assessments.
    """

    score = _assessment_score(candidate)

    if score <= 0:
        return 0.0

    if score >= 90:
        return 1.0

    if score >= 80:
        return 0.85

    if score >= 70:
        return 0.70

    if score >= 60:
        return 0.55

    return 0.35
```

## File: src\scoring\hard_filters.py

- Extension: .py
- Language: python
- Size: 8201 bytes
- Created: 2026-06-26 19:13:40
- Modified: 2026-06-26 19:15:14

### Code

```python
"""
hard_filters.py — instant disqualifiers (score -> 0).

These are cheap rules applied BEFORE feature scoring. They remove ~30,000+
candidates so we never waste compute scoring obvious rejects.

Verified counts against the real 100K file:
    - Honeypot: company start < founding (Krutrim/Sarvam)       -> 73
    - Honeypot: >=3 skills "expert" with duration_months == 0   -> 21
    - Honeypot: experience inflation (claimed YOE >> career sum) -> +24 new
    - Consulting-only careers (TCS/Infosys/Wipro/...)           -> 9,745
    - Foreign (country != India) AND willing_to_relocate False  -> 17,756
    - Non-eng title (Marketing/HR/Sales/...) + AI skills        -> 5,323

API:
    def is_disqualified(candidate: dict, constants) -> tuple[bool, str]:
        '''Return (True, reason) if the candidate should be hard-filtered to 0.'''

NOTE: all accessors are crash-safe — fields may be missing OR explicitly None
(the sandbox accepts uploaded samples, so we never assume clean input).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from src.utils.constants import (
    CONSULTING_FIRMS,
    COMPANY_FOUNDED,
    NON_ENG_TITLES,
    AI_SKILL_TERMS,
    ENG_TITLE_TERMS,
)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Small crash-safe helpers: a key can be ABSENT or present-but-None.
# `dict.get(k, default)` only covers the absent case, so we coerce None too.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _s(value) -> str:
    """Coerce any value to a clean lowercase string (None -> '')."""
    if value is None:
        return ""
    return str(value).strip().lower()


def _list(value) -> list:
    """Coerce to a list (None / non-list -> [])."""
    return value if isinstance(value, list) else []


def _dict(value) -> dict:
    """Coerce to a dict (None / non-dict -> {})."""
    return value if isinstance(value, dict) else {}


def _num(value, default: float = 0.0) -> float:
    """Coerce to a float (None / bad -> default)."""
    try:
        return float(value)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return default


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Honeypots (impossible profiles)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def is_honeypot_company_founding(candidate: dict, company_founded: dict) -> bool:
    """start_year < founded_year for a known-young company."""
    for job in _list(_dict(candidate).get("career_history")):
        company = _s(_dict(job).get("company"))
        if company in company_founded:
            start_date = _s(_dict(job).get("start_date"))
            if not start_date:
                continue
            try:
                start_year = int(start_date[:4])
            except ValueError:
                continue
            if start_year < company_founded[company]:
                return True
    return False


def is_honeypot_zero_duration_expert(candidate: dict, threshold: int = 3) -> bool:
    """>= threshold skills with proficiency == 'expert' AND duration_months == 0."""
    count = 0
    for skill in _list(_dict(candidate).get("skills")):
        skill = _dict(skill)
        if _s(skill.get("proficiency")) == "expert" and _num(skill.get("duration_months")) == 0:
            count += 1
            if count >= threshold:
                return True
    return False


def is_honeypot_experience_inflation(candidate: dict, gap_years: float = 8.0) -> bool:
    """
    Claimed years_of_experience far exceeds the sum of actual career durations.
    Spec honeypot example: "8 years of experience at a company founded 3 years ago".
    """
    profile = _dict(_dict(candidate).get("profile"))
    yoe = _num(profile.get("years_of_experience"))
    total_months = sum(_num(_dict(j).get("duration_months")) for j in _list(_dict(candidate).get("career_history")))
    career_years = total_months / 12.0
    return (yoe - career_years) > gap_years


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Structural disqualifiers
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def is_consulting_only(candidate: dict, consulting_firms: set) -> bool:
    """Every company in career history is a consulting firm."""
    career = _list(_dict(candidate).get("career_history"))
    if not career:
        return False
    for job in career:
        if _s(_dict(job).get("company")) not in consulting_firms:
            return False
    return True


def cannot_relocate(candidate: dict) -> bool:
    """country != 'India' AND willing_to_relocate == False."""
    candidate = _dict(candidate)
    profile = _dict(candidate.get("profile"))
    signals = _dict(candidate.get("redrob_signals") or profile.get("redrob_signals"))
    country = _s(profile.get("country"))
    # Default True (unknown -> don't disqualify); only an explicit False blocks.
    willing = signals.get("willing_to_relocate", True)
    return country != "india" and willing is False


def is_buzzword_stuffer(
    candidate: dict,
    non_eng_titles: set,
    ai_terms: set,
    eng_title_terms: set = ENG_TITLE_TERMS,
) -> bool:
    """
    Non-engineering title + claimed AI skills, with a safety net.

    1. Current title is non-engineering (substring match, e.g. 'senior marketing manager')
    2. AND they list a core AI skill
    3. AND they have NEVER held a real AI/ML engineering JOB TITLE in their history
       -> if they ever held a real AI job title, spare them for scoring.

    Why job titles, not description text? The dataset's stuffers have AI phrases
    deliberately seeded into their summaries/descriptions ("took online courses on
    RAG and vector databases", "LangChain side projects"). Matching description
    text therefore spares everyone. A real AI ENGINEERING JOB TITLE in the career
    history is the one signal these synthetic stuffers never have — and a genuine
    "marketing manager who actually became an AI engineer" would show exactly that.
    (Verified: 0 of the ~5.3K flagged stuffers ever held an AI job title.)
    """
    candidate = _dict(candidate)
    profile = _dict(candidate.get("profile"))
    title = _s(profile.get("current_title"))
    if not title:
        return False

    # (1) substring match: catches "Senior Marketing Manager", "Marketing Manager II", etc.
    if not any(neng in title for neng in non_eng_titles):
        return False

    # (2) do they list a core AI skill?
    has_ai_skill = any(_s(_dict(s).get("name")) in ai_terms for s in _list(candidate.get("skills")))
    if not has_ai_skill:
        return False

    # (3) safety net: did they EVER hold a real AI/ML engineering job title?
    # (the unfakeable signal — descriptions/summaries are seeded with AI buzzwords)
    for job in _list(candidate.get("career_history")):
        job_title = _s(_dict(job).get("title"))
        if job_title and any(term in job_title for term in eng_title_terms):
            return False

    return True


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Master gate
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def is_disqualified(candidate: dict, constants=None) -> "tuple[bool, str]":
    """Master gate. Returns (True, reason) if the candidate must score 0."""
    if is_honeypot_company_founding(candidate, COMPANY_FOUNDED):
        return True, "Impossible company joining date"

    if is_honeypot_zero_duration_expert(candidate):
        return True, "Fake expert skills"

    if is_honeypot_experience_inflation(candidate):
        return True, "Impossible experience inflation"

    if is_consulting_only(candidate, CONSULTING_FIRMS):
        return True, "Consulting-only career"

    if cannot_relocate(candidate):
        return True, "Cannot relocate"

    if is_buzzword_stuffer(candidate, NON_ENG_TITLES, AI_SKILL_TERMS):
        return True, "Buzzword stuffer"

    return False, ""

```

"""
reasoning.py — generates the 1-2 sentence reasoning string per candidate.

This is the Stage-4 make-or-break. The organizer validator/reviewer PENALIZES:
    - empty reasoning
    - all-identical strings

    - hallucinated skills/companies not in the profile

"""
constants.py — shared lookups used across filters / features / penalties.

These are starting points derived from the JD and the verified dataset. Tune as
needed; thresholds live in config/weights.yaml.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

# --- Consulting firms (JD explicit disqualifier when career is consulting-ONLY) ---
CONSULTING_FIRMS = {
    "tcs", "tata consultancy services", "infosys", "wipro", "accenture",
    "cognizant", "capgemini", "tech mahindra", "hcl", "hcl technologies",
    "mindtree", "ltimindtree", "mphasis", "deloitte",
}

# --- Known young companies: start_date before founding => honeypot ---
# Verified: 38 Krutrim + 36 Sarvam impossible profiles in the real data.
COMPANY_FOUNDED = {
    "krutrim": 2023,      # founded Dec 2023
    "sarvam": 2023,       # founded 2023
    "sarvam ai": 2023,
    # extend with any other young companies you find
}

# --- Non-engineering current/primary titles (stuffer trap when paired w/ AI skills) ---
NON_ENG_TITLES = {
    "marketing manager", "sales executive", "hr manager", "graphic designer",
    "content writer", "accountant", "civil engineer", "mechanical engineer",
    "operations manager", "customer support", "project manager",
    "business analyst", "recruiter",
}

# --- Terms that indicate a real ML/AI/DS engineering title ---
ENG_TITLE_TERMS = {
    "ml engineer", "machine learning", "ai engineer", "applied scientist",
    "data scientist", "nlp engineer", "research engineer", "ai research engineer",
    "software engineer", "backend engineer", "data engineer", "analytics engineer",
}

# --- Core AI skill names (used to detect buzzword stuffing on non-eng titles) ---
AI_SKILL_TERMS = {
    "pytorch", "tensorflow", "llm", "fine-tuning llms", "nlp", "machine learning",
    "deep learning", "transformers", "rag", "vector database", "embeddings",
}

# --- Retrieval / ranking / IR evidence terms (the JD's "absolutely need" core) ---
RETRIEVAL_TERMS = {
    "retrieval", "ranking", "recommendation", "recommender", "search",
    "embedding", "embeddings", "vector", "semantic", "information retrieval",
    "relevance", "nlp", "natural language", "bm25", "learning to rank",
    "faiss", "pinecone", "weaviate", "qdrant", "milvus", "elasticsearch", "opensearch",
}

# --- Production-evidence terms (research-only check) ---
PRODUCTION_TERMS = {
    "production", "deployed", "shipped", "users", "scale", "latency",
    "a/b test", "real users", "live",
}

# --- NLP/IR terms (CV/speech/robotics-only check: present => NOT cv-only) ---
NLP_IR_TERMS = {
    "nlp", "retrieval", "ranking", "text", "language", "search",
    "information retrieval", "recommendation",
}

# --- Indian Tier-1 cities welcomed by the JD ---
PREFERRED_CITIES = {"noida", "pune", "hyderabad", "mumbai", "delhi", "delhi ncr", "bangalore", "bengaluru"}

# --- Example product companies (bonus signal) ---
PRODUCT_COMPANIES = {
    "swiggy", "zomato", "cred", "razorpay", "flipkart", "phonepe", "meesho",
    "ola", "uber", "google", "meta", "amazon", "microsoft", "myntra",
    "sharechat", "dream11", "groww", "navi",
}
